MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

Industry View In-Line
We keep our 3Q26 iPhone build estimate unchanged at 54M units (+4% q/q, -2%
y/y). But we have made adjustments to new iPhone 18 builds by lowering iPhone
Duo by 0.5M units to 0.5M, while raising iPhone 18 Pro Max by 0.5M units to 8.5M.

For 4Q, we lower our iPhone build estimate by 1M units to 72M units (+33% q/q,
-5% y/y), reflecting lower legacy iPhone models (iPhone 16/17e). Total new iPhone
18 model family units remains at 71M units (-21% y/y) in 2H26, with one less SKU
launched compared to last year. We continue to expect 7M units of iPhone Duo
builds in 2H26. With iPhone Duo sales starting soon, we will continue to monitor
ramp-up progress for any upside or downside to builds. Our revised full-year iPhone

build forecast implies CY26 shipment at 234M units (+3% y/y).
Our 3Q26 iPad build forecast is lowered by 0.5M units to 11.5M units (-12% q/q, -
18% y/y). We lowered 3Q entry level iPad builds due to  pull-forward demand in 1H
and the impact of rising prices. We also lower our 4Q iPad build forecast by 0.7M
units to 10.3M units (-10% q/q, -26% y/y). Our revised full-year iPad build forecast
implies CY26 shipments at 46.8M units (-14% y/y).

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

and mSAP demand expansion
While most investors have focused primarily on ABF substrate opportunities over the
past 6+ months, we believe BT substrate products will also benefit from a multi-year
pricing uptrend. Incorporating our revised BT substrate TAM growth and a more
constructive outlook for the mSAP industry, we raise our TPs for ZDT/Kinsus/NYPCB to

upward revisions to our 2027/28E earnings forecasts. For Unimicron, as the company is

gradually phasing capacity out of the BT substrate segment, we adjust our TP more
modestly from NT$1,220 to NT$1,270 on the back of 2% 2027-28E earnings hike.

### Page 4

BT substrate supply/demand gap at 11%/14% in 2027/28E on the back of solid memory demand

We introduce our 2026-28E BT substrate TAM forecast, expanding at a 32% 2026-28E 
CAGR to reach US$16.8bn in 2028E (up from US$7.4bn in 2025; Exhibit 1). This growth is 
primarily driven by solid memory demand in general servers (general servers will account 
for 32% of total BT TAM in 2028E, up from 21% in 2025; Exhibit 2), with increasing 
memory capacity/BT content per server. We expect the memory BT substrate TAM to 
outpace the broader BT substrate TAM with a 41% 2026-28E CAGR, accounting for 77% 
of total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (was 56% in 2025). 
On the other hand, we expect the capacity growth rate to decelerate vs. the previous 
upcycle in 2020-22 (6% 2026-28E CAGR vs. 9% 2020-22 CAGR), as key Asian substrate 
suppliers are placing greater focus on their ABF substrate business, and are hence 
scaling back their BT substrate capacity (including Unimicron, which held 8% market 
share in 2025). 
As such, we now expect the overall BT substrate supply/demand gap to reach 
1%/11%/14% in 2H26/2027/2028 (Exhibit 3), while major BT suppliers have started to 
reduce capacity from 2026, potentially leading to a supply shortage in 2H26. We expect 
BT capacity utilization rates (UTR) to trend up amid a supply-constrained environment 
(surpassing 100% from 2027E per GSe), providing a better BT pricing environment going 
forward. The BT substrate supply tightness since the start of the year has directly led to 
a 10% YTD increase in overall BT substrate pricing, and we believe pricing momentum 
for BT substrates will remain robust in 2H26-2028E. 
Accordingly, we forecast overall BT substrate prices will further increase by 
15%-20%/10%-15% in 2027/28E, benefiting BT substrates’ profitability/pricing for 
Taiwanese players (GMs ranged from 15%-27% in 2Q26 and are projected to increase to 
18%-34%/19%-36%+ in 2027/28E; Exhibit 4).

Solid growth in general server BT substrate TAM will support mid- to long-term BT 
market growth 
We expect general server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 42% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.3bn in 2028E (up from US$1.6bn in 2025; Exhibit 8), which will account for 32% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 21% in 2025). We expect general server 
memory BT substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 43% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$5.0bn in 2028E (up from US$1.4bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total memory BT 
substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 20% in 2025). This is driven by solid general server 
shipments (we expect general server shipments to register an 8% 2026-28E CAGR; see 
here; Exhibit 9) alongside increasing memory capacity/BT content per general server 
(Exhibit 10). 
Our US analyst, James Schneider, expects Intel/AMD general server CPU shipment to 
grow at a 28%/36% 2026-28E CAGR, reaching a combined 59mn units in 2028E (vs. 
26mn units in 2025). This supports our constructive view that general servers are the key 
driver of the current BT substrate upcycle, given the strong correlation between memory 
BT substrate TAM growth and general server CPU shipment growth.

### Page 8

Strong AI server shipment growth in 2026-28E will also support BT TAM expansion 
We expect AI server BT substrate TAM to deliver a 73% 2026-28E CAGR and reach 
US$1,476mn in 2028E (up from US$160mn in 2025; Exhibit 14), accounting for 9% of 
total BT substrate TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). We expect AI server memory BT 
substrate TAM to grow slightly faster at a 75% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$1,428mn 
in 2028E (up from US$145mn in 2025), accounting for 9% of total memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2028E (up from 2% in 2025). This is driven by solid growth in AI server shipments, 
as we expect AI servers (8-GPU equivalent) and AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent) to 
register 17%/71% 2026-28E CAGRs (see here) with increasing memory capacity per 
server/per server rack. 
We expect GPU/CPU attach rates to move from 2x to 1.1-1.4x over time as agentic AI 
applications become mainstream, implying that each GPU deployment will require more 
server CPUs (see our US analyst Jim Schneider’s Intel initiation). Given that memory BT 
content in AI servers is primarily tied to server CPU count, if the GPU/CPU attach rate 
narrows from 2.0x to 1.1-1.4x, we estimate 1%/1%-2% upside to memory BT substrate 
TAM in 2027/28E. 
For AI server racks (72-GPU equivalent), BT content per rack continues to expand mainly 
due to increasing demand for more memory capacity, resulting in a 50% 2026-28E 
CAGR in BT content per AI server rack (Exhibit 15). A key upgrade is that Nvidia’s VR200 
will support higher DRAM capacity, implying a 100% increase in DRAM memory BT 
substrate content compared to GB200/GB300. We also note Nvidia is releasing a 
standalone Vera CPU rack designed for agentic AI, implying further demand upside for 
AI CPU shipments and memory BT substrates, as each Vera CPU rack incorporates 8x 
more DRAM memory BT substrate content compared to VR200.

### Page 9

Smartphones remain critical to BT demand with growth going forward tracking 
slower than the overall BT industry 
We expect overall smartphone BT substrate TAM to grow at a 24% 2026-28E CAGR 
(tracking slower than the overall BT industry CAGR of 32%) to reach US$6.5bn in 2028E 
(up from US$3.8bn in 2025), accounting for 39% of total 2028E BT TAM (down from 
51% in 2025). We expect smartphone memory BT substrate TAM to grow at a faster 36% 
2026-28E CAGR to reach US$2.4bn in 2028E (up from US$1.0bn in 2025), accounting 
for 19% of total memory BT TAM in 2028E (down from 24% in 2025). 
As mentioned in our previous update on the BT substrate industry (see here), we believe 
the previous upcycle (in 2020-22) was mainly driven by the proliferation of SiP/AiP 
designs that increased BT substrate content per device, especially for smartphones. 
While smartphones remain critical to overall BT demand, accounting for 51% of total 
2025 BT substrate TAM, we expect their contribution to moderate going forward 
(reaching 39% of 2028E BT TAM). This is driven by softer smartphone shipment volumes 
(we now forecast smartphone shipments at -10%/+2%/+2% YoY in 2026-28E; see here) 
and the fact that BT substrate content per smartphone will be mainly driven by higher 
memory BT substrate pricing (Exhibit 16) rather than other meaningful content value 
gains (memory will account for 46% of smartphone BT ASP in 2028E, up from 32% in 
2025). 
We expect 25%/26%/28% of overall smartphone shipments to be equipped with 
mmWave connectivity in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 17), resulting in an 18% 2026-28E CAGR 
for smartphone AiP BT TAM to reach US$1,128mn in 2028 (up from US$713mn in 2025). 
For iPhones, mmWave connectivity is only available for models sold in the US 
(representing 35%-40% of total iPhone shipments per GSe). In the Android camp, 
Samsung’s flagship smartphones that support mmWave connectivity are also only for 
the US market.

Automotive BT consumption to steadily increase in coming years 
We expect automotive BT TAM to grow at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR and reach US$777mn 
in 2028E (up from US$317mn in 2025), which will account for 5% of total 2028E BT TAM 
(up from 4% in 2025). This stronger-than-industry growth is mainly driven by rising EV 
penetration going forward (EV penetration rate will increase from 14% in 2025 to 20% in 
2028E per our Japanese auto team; Exhibit 19) and the corresponding increase in 
electronics content per car, which requires more BT substrates. Furthermore, we believe 
rising autonomous driving penetration will result in an increase in electronic 
components/BT consumption per car.

tracking faster at a 34% 2026-28E CAGR. We believe this lower ratio of BT market

previous upcycle

Unimicron transferring BT substrate capacity to high-end large-body-size ABF 
substrates (see here), while SEMCO is focusing more on expanding its ABF substrate 
capacity (see here) with its BT substrate business mainly focusing on memory and

### Page 14

We have seen BT substrate demand improve since the start of 2026 alongside a firmer 
pricing outlook, and we expect demand growth to consistently outpace supply in 
2026-28E (demand CAGR of 15% vs. supply CAGR of 6%). We believe industry utilization 
rates (UTR) will continue to trend up over the coming years (reaching 100%+ after 
2027E) due to slow supply growth going forward. 
Memory predominantly requires wire bonding chip scale package (WBCSP) technology, 
and this production capacity is not interchangeable with flip-chip chip-scale package 
(FCCSP) technology (where smartphone APs represent the biggest demand driver). As 
such, we believe memory BT substrate supply will remain tight through at least 1H28, as 
new dedicated memory capacity will only come online in 2028. Overall BT pricing has 
already risen by 10% YTD, partially driven by higher material costs (including substrate 
CCL, copper foil, gold, etc., which account for 60% of total BT substrate BOM; Exhibit 
26). Going forward, we believe the slow increase in BT substrate supply, especially 
WBCSP capacity, will be the key price hike driver rather than simply reflecting the 
material cost increase. Furthermore, BT substrates typically account for less than 5% of 
total memory BOM, providing ample room for further BT price hikes amid the rising 
memory pricing trend.

### Page 15

With favorable BT substrate supply/demand conditions, we believe the profitability of 
key BT vendors will see a structural increase in the coming years. Similar to current 
dynamics in the ABF market, the BT substrate industry’s profitability profile has 
navigated three upcycles and three downcycles over the past 20 years (Exhibit 27). 
Overall margin profiles for key BT vendors have begun improving since the start of 2026, 
supported by increasing memory demand amid a supply-constrained environment, 
especially following several years of slow capex investment. We believe this profitability 
enhancement will continue in 2H26-2028, supported by sustained industry demand 
uptrend.

### Page 16

Optical module mSAP is seeing a widening S/D gap, prompting a shift of BT capacity to meet strong 
demand

We introduce our 2026-28E 800G+ optical module mSAP TAM forecast, expanding at an 
87% 2026-28E CAGR to reach US$7.1bn by 2028E (up from US$0.5bn in 2025; Exhibit 
28). This is based on our 800G+ optical module shipment estimates of a 48% 2026-28E 
CAGR (see here). The connectivity PCB within the 800G+ optical module requires mSAP 
technology, where several PCB makers have exposure to and are able to leverage their 
extensive mSAP experience from smartphone substrate-like PCB (SLP) production. 
On the other hand, major PCB and substrate suppliers are placing greater focus on this 
emerging business and pursuing proactive capacity expansion plans, especially ZDT and 
Compeq (the latter aims to ramp optical module mSAP capacity to reach monthly 
revenue of ~NT$3bn by end 2027 vs. less than NT$100mn as of 2Q26; see here). 
Although Unimicron was a front-runner in the 800G+ optical module mSAP business in 
2025 (29% market share in 2025 per GSe), the company is placing greater focus on its 
ABF substrate business, and hence leaving room for emerging peers to capture market 
share through aggressive expansion plans and technology leadership. 
We believe ZDT will become the market share leader in the optical module mSAP 
industry, as we expect the company to expand mSAP capacity by 5x over the next two 
years, positioning it well to gain share in a supply-constrained environment (our industry 
checks indicate mSAP is the most supply-constrained component in the 1.6T optical 
transceiver supply chain). Furthermore, the company currently holds an industry-leading 
yield of 90%+ for its optical module mSAP products. 
Overall, we expect the 800G+ optical module mSAP supply/demand gap to reach 
5%/25%/38% in 2026/27/28E (Exhibit 29). Although major mSAP suppliers continue 
to have proactive expansion plans, this appears insufficient to keep pace with strong 
demand. The 800G+ optical module mSAP tightness since the start of 2026 has directly 
led to a 20%-30% pricing increase YTD, and we believe the pricing outlook for 800G+ 
optical module mSAP will remain solid in 2027/28E.

### Page 23

Investment thesis, PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - Zhen Ding Technology 
Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China; it has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market compared 
to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% ABF 
substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the most 
from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025-27E CAGR) due to 
the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. We also 
expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to drive 
ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 39% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We see 
further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due to 
our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.  
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$1,025 is based on a SOTP valuation methodology, 
including (1) 3.5x blended 2028E P/B for ABF & BT substrate, (2) 20x 2028E P/E for 
high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x 
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle 
average valuation). 
Key downside risks include: (1) weaker-than-expected iPhone demand or slower 
content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate 
ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

### Page 24

Investment Thesis - Kinsus
Kinsus is one of the key IC substrates suppliers globally with ~10% ABF/BT substrates
market share in 2024, with ~18% of total 2025 revenue contributed by its contact lens
subsidiary, Pegavision (6941.TW, Not Covered). With our view that the ABF substrate
shortage started from 2Q26 (ABF substrate return to supply demand balance from late
2025), we believe Kinsus’ performance will continue to improve compared with high-end

ABF peers considering the company’s increasing market share in the high-end ABF
substrate market; however, ~36% of the company’s revenue was contributed by BT
substrates in 2025 and 30% of the company’s ABF revenue is from non-LTA customers,
which we believe will continue to enjoy a better pricing outlook. As a result, we believe
Kinsus’ earnings outlook should continue to be strong (~118% 2026-29E CAGR), mainly
driven by better BT substrate and non-LTA ABF substrate pricing outlook, despite the

unfavorable demand outlook before 1H26. We believe Kinsus is an experienced
substrate supplier with a clear capacity expansion plan that can also upgrade its product
portfolio in the long term. We believe we are at the beginning of an industry upcycle,
making us positive on Kinsus’ stock performance over the next 12 months.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Kinsus
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,515 for Kinsus is based on

19.5x 2028E P/E, which is +2.0x STDV higher than Kinsus’ past upcycle average P/E
(14.2x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, (3)
Slower/Less-than-expected AI server orders from 2H26.
Investment Thesis - NYPCB
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB

considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s

high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148%

2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s
earnings and stock performance.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - NYPCB
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$2,700 for NYPCB is based
on 21x 2028E P/E, which is +1.5x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E (13.3x).
Key downside risks: (1) Slower-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)

Slower-than-expected ABF substrates pricing upgrading outlook, and (3)
Slower-than-expected NYPCB new high-end capacity qualification progress.
Investment Thesis - Unimicron Technology
Unimicron is a key supplier of ABF substrates (25% market share in 2025) and BT
substrates and also produces FPC/HDI/PCB products. We are Neutral rated on the
shares given long-term growth opportunity prospects: (1) over 70% of ABF shipments
are covered by long-term agreements (LTA), which limits pricing flexibility and makes

price hike less likely; (2) the company’s execution track record has been weaker than
peers; (3) capacity expansion plan remains slower than peers, constraining its ability to
capture incremental AI demand. The current ABF substrates market is seeing more
favorable supply demand outlook, and Unimicron is likely to see better pricing outlook
to reflect rising material costs in the coming quarters. AI ABF account for 10% of the
company’s total revenue, but the growth is likely to be slower than peers due to the

company continuing to lose market share to peers given its less favourable production
yield and slow capacity expansion. Overall, we are Neutral rated on Unimicron given our
outlook for limited upside potential, barring ASIC AI servers, despite our generally
positive view in the long term on its profitability trajectory.
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Unimicron Technology
Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,270 for Unimicron is

based on 17.4x 2028E P/E, which is 2.7x STDV higher than the past upcycle average P/E
(10.3x).
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Slower/faster-than-expected PC demand recovery, (2)
Slower/faster-than-expected ABF substrates upgrading pace, and (3) Slower/faster
than-expected AI server PCB market share loss progress.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

## Document 125: 3443 Nomura20261019

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
Global Unichip Corp   3443.TW 3443 TT
Global Markets Research
19 October 2026
Riding the next megatrend of SoIC; maintain Buy
Several key CSP projects in the pipeline to support growth until 2028-29F
Benefiting from SoIC — the next megatrend in advanced packaging, in our view
GUC shares have rallied ~100% since late July (vs the TAIEX up 11%), driven by a strong 
wave of consensus earnings upward revisions (Fig. 11). This is largely in line with our 
observations highlighted in our 2 Aug report that “consensus expectations were way too low.” 
We believe the Street is starting to acknowledge that there is a strong pipeline of CSP projects 
over the next 2-3 years, including Google CPU (GOOGL US, Not rated; revenue sharply 
higher in 2026 with momentum potentially sustaining into 2027F and 2028F as Axion 3 
doubles silicon content from Axion 2), as well as the other top three US hyperscalers’ ASIC 
and CPU projects along with Tesla’s (TSLA US, Not rated) AI5/AI6.5. GUC not only has 
secured projects from top US hyperscalers, but at the same time acquired additional 
meaningful customers with volume globally, e.g. China auto ASIC and US AI ASIC using a 
SoW approach. However, among all, the one that has caught our attention the most is the 
Google TPU v.10 compute die opportunity (though we believe the turnkey deal is yet to be 
finalized) — not only because the addressable size could be significant, but this business 
opportunity might originate from GUC’s G-link 3D die-on-die IP. (Fig. 1 Fig. 4) We raise our 
2026-28F EPS for GUC by 15%-37% to incorporate business upside from non-Axion projects, 
and raise TP to TWD9,600 (from TWD5,800), based on an unchanged 40x target P/E, while 
rolling forward valuation to the 2027-28F average EPS. We are yet to incorporate overseas 
convertible bonds (ECB) in our model/EPS estimates.
The rising importance of SoIC in advanced packaging should benefit GUC, given its 
leading investments in Glink-3D die-on-die IP among design service peers
SoIC is not new, but it was small before 2026 given limited adoption by AMD MI300 chip (AMD 
US, Not rated) and Apple M-series chip (AAPL US, Not rated). In 2026, nVidia’s (NVDA US, Not 
rated) demand for CPO using TSMC’s (2330 TT, Buy) COUPE (EIC on PIC stacking) has further 
lifted SoIC demand. In our June anchor report, we noted that nVidia’s Feynman could become 
the first GPU-on-GPU stacking SoIC (along with SiC carrier) AI chip. Since then, several US CSP 
ASIC projects have also planned to enable SoIC die stacking in their chip roadmap, according to 
our industry checks. GUC’s Glink-3D IP (see Fig. 1- Fig. 4) appears well positioned to ride this 
trend as it has developed this IP along with TSMC’s roadmap and currently a number of CSPs 
are using its IP to build their test chip – which in the long run could prove to be a good stepping 
stone for GUC to win more services, in our view. Besides, GUC’s SoIC IP would also be 
deployed with TSMC’s scale-up CPO customers (e.g. AyarLabs, Fig. 5).

### Page 3

Company profile
Established in 1998, GUC is one of the leading design service companies in Taiwan with self-developed IPs. TSMC is GUC's single-largest
shareholder with a 35% share.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD9,600 is based on 40x 2027-28F average EPS (40x P/E is its historical mid level). The benchmark index for the stock is TAIEX.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks include 1) fierce ASIC competition, 2) slowdown of key projects and lack of capacity, and 3) more insourcing trend from customers

ESG
GUC has six major ESG strategies to achieve the goals of sustainable development, including corporate governance, innovative R&D, partnership
prosperity, environmental sustainability, friendly workplace, and social participation.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

研 究  報 告                
貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異 
營收 1,630 1,613 +1% 
稅前淨利 394 417 -5.5% 
稅後淨利 326 333 -2.2% 
EPS(元) 2.28 2.34 -2.6% 
資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

表二、研究部預估調整 
資料來源： 華星光自結、統一研究部預估

## Document 170: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 252: ECB caught in the French drama

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECB caught in the French drama.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The European Central Bank meeting on 29 October is not
expected to result in an interest rate hike. Instead, the French
crisis is increasingly eroding the need for additional rate hikes
Carsten Brzeski
With the surge in bond yields on the back of the crisis in France, we expect the
ECB to remain on hold at next week's meeting
Back in September, the ECB surprised markets with a more hawkish
message, at least between the lines. With headline inﬂation higher

for longer and core inﬂation expected to increase in 2027, the stage
seemed set for at least one more rate hike at the December
meeting. Some even speculated about a back-to-back increase in
October.
One month later, and after a severe ﬁscal and political crisis in
France, the ECB is unlikely to repeat the hawkish tone at next week’s
meeting. At face value, macro developments since the last meeting
would still justify another rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased

again in September and most leading indicators point to continued
resilience in the eurozone economy. However, the surge in bond
Cookie settings

### Page 2

yields on the back of the French crisis is already imposing a new
restrictive monetary policy stance. In fact, the surge in German
bond yields since the September meeting has already had roughly
the same impact on growth and inﬂation as an additional 25bp
rate hike. For France, it's closer to a 50bp hike. Or in other words, the
bond market has taken over the ECB’s job.
All of this means that the ECB is very likely to stay on hold next
week and will delay any next rate hike decision to December. This is

when new staﬀ projections will be available and when there will
also be more insights into whether higher energy prices will
eventually bring knock-on eﬀects on the rest of the economy or
not. In fact, there are three main parameters that, in our view, will
determine the ECB’s path going forward: i) the length and intensity
of the war in the Middle East. The longer oil prices remain elevated,
the higher the probability of more rate hikes; ii) the knock-on eﬀects

of higher energy prices. So far, there has hardly been any pass-
through of higher energy prices in the rest of the economy. If this
remains the case, the need for further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t,
the likelihood of new rate hikes goes up. iii) France. Any solution to
the current French ﬁscal woes could bring some relief to bond
markets and allow the ECB to continue hiking rates. Without any
solution, bond markets have taken over the ECB’s role, making rate

hikes less likely (and less needed).
Because it's France
Even if France does not play a role in the ECB’s policy statement
next week, it will be an important element at the press conference.
Political and ﬁscal concerns in France, and the resulting widening in
French spreads, have raised questions about whether the ECB could
intervene through the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI),
which allows it to purchase securities issued by member states

whose ﬁnancing conditions have deteriorated for reasons not
justiﬁed by country-speciﬁc fundamentals. However, before
activating it, the Governing Council considers a cumulative set of
criteria to determine whether the countries concerned are pursuing
sound and sustainable ﬁscal and macroeconomic policies. In
practice, this includes compliance with the EU ﬁscal framework,

### Page 3

which would, at a minimum, require the proposed French budget to
be fully adopted.
The ECB has nevertheless left itself some discretion, stating that the
intervention criteria “will be dynamically adjusted to the unfolding
risks and conditions to be addressed”. This brings back memories of
the famous “because it’s France” by former European Commission
President Jean-Claude Juncker. Generally speaking, the ECB will be
in a diﬃcult position: intervening too quickly in support of France

may be viewed unfavourably by other member states and
interpreted as monetary ﬁnancing of the debt of a country
unwilling to follow the rules. That argues strongly against early
intervention. Also, don’t forget that the leadership changes at the
ECB over the next months (Schnabel, Lane, Lagarde) could also
aﬀect how the ECB views TPI.
Given the challenges of justifying the activation of TPI at present, we
expect the ECB to adopt a three-pronged approach to address the

French crisis.
1. The ﬁrst line of defence is a more dovish approach to policy
rates. As bond markets are currently taking over the ECB’s job
of inserting a more restrictive monetary stance on the
economy, there is less need for the ECB to hike rates
aggressively, or to hike rates at all. In fact, the ECB has already
started to prepare for this option, as ECB president Christine
Lagarde recently noted that “while growth has been resilient,
since our last meeting long-term interest rates have risen

notably, which will slow growth and reduce pass-through by
more than projected in our September exercise".
2. The second line of defence would be a covert return to bond
purchases. The ECB could instead pause quantitative tightening
temporarily and reinvest maturing bonds in its portfolio
'ﬂexibly', sending a positive signal to bond markets. This had
already happened during the Covid period.
3. Last but not least, should contagion in eurozone bond markets

intensify, we expect the ECB to eventually trigger the TPI. As
long as France remains non-compliant with the European ﬁscal

All in all, next week’s ECB meeting looks set to be uneventful on
action but clearly not on words.

## Document 263: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in
services business confidence
September 2026: Business climate in the
manufacturing sector comfortably in the
expansion zone for the 8th month in a row
September 2026: Strengthening inflationary
pressures
September 2026: Slight further increase in

September 2026: Marked increase in inflation,

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Source PDF: all report pdf/Federal Reserve preview_ A pause before a December hike.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Despite hot inﬂation prints in September and the Fed having
signalled that further action to tame inﬂation will likely be
needed, markets and economists are united in expecting the
central bank to leave interest rates on hold at the 28 October
FOMC meeting. Ongoing concerns about elevated price
pressures mean we do expect a December interest rate rise

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
The Fed looks set to hold rates steady at the October FOMC meeting before
hiking again in December
Fed ofﬁcials play for time
The immediate reaction to the surprise September 0.3% month-on-
month core CPI print was to send the chances of an October rate
hike above 50%. But this didn’t last long. Earlier comments from
Federal Reserve Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and Michelle Bowman
and Governor Chris Waller, signalling no rush to decide on the next
policy move, quickly calmed markets. The fact that Fed Chair Kevin
Warsh subsequently chose not to talk up the chances of action
when he spoke at the IMF meetings in Bangkok, unlike at the

Jackson Hole Conference before the September hike, further

While it is true to say that recent jobs numbers have been
disappointing and interest rate-sensitive sectors, such as the
housing market, are experiencing increasing pain, the economy is
growing robustly, and inﬂation remains above target. Fed oﬃcials
themselves have made it clear that they think more tightening of
monetary policy is going to be required to bring inﬂation to heel.

Given that the latest Federal Reserve Summary of Economic
Projections showed four FOMC members projecting 50bp of rate
hikes between now and the end of the year, the decision is unlikely
to be unanimous. Beth Hammack, Neel Kashkari and Lorie Logan
all voted to hike in July when the rest of the committee wanted to
hold steady. All three could again dissent in favour of hiking.
The press release is likely to be brief, and we also expect another
short and snappy press conference from Chair Warsh. He's likely to

keep things quite matter-of-fact given his lack of enthusiasm for
forward guidance. We expect his message to be that the economy
remains in decent shape, unemployment is low, and inﬂation is
above target. The policy changes being made will be determined
by the trends in incoming data, with oﬃcials currently thinking that
they probably have more work to do.

Looking towards December
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after
the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,

meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate hike at the December meeting
(leaving the Fed funds target rate range at 4-4.25%) followed by
a prolonged pause through to December 2027, when we have that
December 2026 hike being reversed. This is in line with the current
consensus forecast, while the Fed’s own projections (its central
forecast) see one hike this year with rates left unchanged
throughout 2027.
While the sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer

dollar are tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, our forecast
path for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following
the midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow
of energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, also allows for a
sharp reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point
that is crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when
reﬁneries are working at capacity. If correct, this should

meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and with US wage growth
(the biggest cost input for corporate America) looking very benign
at just 3% and the largest component of the inﬂation basket –
housing at 35% by weight – set to experience more disinﬂation, we

The risk to our view is clearly tilted towards a longer-lasting conﬂict
rather than one that is resolved imminently. This means we would
not rule out additional hike(s) in 1Q 2027, but with households
experiencing a greater squeeze on spending power (weak wage
growth amid rising energy costs) and bond yields likely pushing
even higher – leading to new cycle highs for mortgage rates and

corporate borrowing costs – the risks of a weaker economy will
increase. As such, in this more stressed economic scenario,
eventual cuts to policy rates will likely be swifter and more
substantial in 2H 2027 than in our base case.
The Fed to leave well enough alone and let
Treasuries do their own thing
A solid performance for US Treasuries in the past week has acted to
reduce the prior tightening impulse that had gripped the market.
The 10yr yield below 5% obviously contrasts dramatically with the

prior break above 5.25%. We’re not convinced the more recent
downward lurch is persisting. But its genesis lends itself to a softer
approach from the FOMC at this meeting.
The risk-oﬀ tone creates a heightened risk proﬁle for the US
economy, raising the probability that the Fed holds at this meeting.
The battering that the French bond market has been taking is
another important impulse, as it heightens the broader sense of
bond angst. And at the margin, it can add to systemic pressure.

This is not the type of environment where the Fed would be inclined
to add to concerns by tightening policy. It’s the perfect meeting to
keep rates on hold.
Whether we remain below 5% for the 10yr yield is a whole other
question. We're minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still
have inﬂation, ﬁscal deﬁcit, and AI-boom impulses at play. The Fed
will no doubt remind us of these ongoing pressures. Plus, a break
back above 5% will likely need a degree of calm in the risk space

ﬁrst. We think it should ultimately move back above 5%; the timing,

We’re likely still some way from the Fed spelling out a plan to shrink

Warsh FOMC meetings have set the dollar
tone since June
The dollar should be able to hold on to most of its September
gains, as Fed communication is likely to remain supportive of
higher US yields. If the dollar is to correct lower, a renewed tech-led
risk-oﬀ move rather than a dovish shift in Fed messaging is the
more likely catalyst.
After a sharp 4% run higher through September and into early
October, the dollar has entered a more consolidative phase. Rate

diﬀerentials are the most important driver at the moment, and
these have moved slightly against the dollar this month as the US
tech sell-oﬀ has pared back Fed tightening expectations.
Broadening out the lens, it looks like Warsh-led FOMC meetings
have set the four-to-six-week trend for the dollar this year. June’s
meeting was hawkish, July’s was seen as dovish, and the Fed
delivered a hawkish hike in September.
We suspect that Warsh has learned from the criticism he received

over July's FOMC meeting, where he implied that the bond market
was doing the tightening (read as the Fed would not have to). That
probably means he will not be drawn into concluding that wider
credit spreads and softer equity prices mean tighter ﬁnancial
conditions now require fewer Fed hikes.
Assuming that Warsh sticks to the script that monetary policy
credibility remains the Fed’s top priority, short-dated US yields can
remain well-supported, and the dollar can largely hold on to the

gains it has made since early September. And the euro looks like it

Source PDF: all report pdf/How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: DB Research

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.
Investors
are less
rational

are more
rational

Emotions cooled as ‘rational’ investing took over
Lower realised volatility in markets in Q3 helped clam emotional market responses. That indicates investors are
detaching from the volatile macro and geopolitical backdrop
Emotion dipped markedly in Q3 Month-by-month
July 2026: A fear-driven sell-off in

semiconductor stocks, particularly in
Korea, was balanced by optimism from
strong earnings reports from major tech
firms and a significant drop in oil prices.
August 2026: An easing in inflation
pressures lowered expectations of Fed
rate hikes and supported a rally in
Treasury markets. Strong corporate
earnings season and signs of de-
escalation in the Middle East
September 2026: Rising energy prices
and renewed uncertainty in the Middle
East fuelled a sell-off in global sovereign

bonds. Concerns about debt sustainability
and hawkish central bank commentary
weighed on equity markets despite some
encouraging economic signals.

### Page 9

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain blockades3 4

### Page 11

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle Power Countries" 1 2

### Page 12

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche BankResearch Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs. consumer strain3 4

The impact of today’s themes 1 year ahead:
We asked our AI model, dbLumina, to analyse the impact of the themes for the quarter. The ‘positive’ and ‘negative’ indicators are
relative to expectations at the beginning of the quarter. For example, a net negative score for the S&P 500 does not mean we expect

the index to fall. Rather it means we see the rising probability that returns will be lower than previously expected.

The impact of today’s themes 3 years ahead:
The medium-term picture is far more optimistic than the short-term picture. The implicit assumption is that the macro and geopolitical
volatility that is currently weighing on markets will either resolve itself over the coming 3 years or become normalised enou gh that the

underlying earnings power of the US economy will drive equity markets ahead and, to a lesser extent, economy growth.

With the ongoing major bond selloff, we asked respondents to rank what they
thought were the most important drivers. Inflation, fiscal fears, and stronger-than-
expected US growth were picked as the most important drivers. 30% picked
inflation as the most important driver even though 10yr US Breakevens have hardly

moved in the selloff.
Thinking about the recent rise in US Treasury yields, please rank the following factors in order of importance,
from the most important driver to the least important. Please drag and drop the factors into the order that
best represents your view of their importance.
Source: dbDataInsights Survey, Deutsche Bank Research

To highlight the inconsistency, US inflation expectations over the next 5 years
have risen only marginally since we last asked in June, back up to 2.9% from 2.8%.
5yr European inflation expectations are unchanged at 2.4%. So while there is a
strong narrative that inflation is causing the bond selloff, it doesn’t stack up with
long-term inflation expectations.

Despite US Treasury yields reaching multi-decade highs in September, most
respondents expect the peak in rates to be higher over the next 12 months.
The average expected peak in the 10-year yield is 5.70%, with most expecting
around 5.5% but with a longish tail and a secondary peak around 6%.
Over the next 12 months, what do you expect will be the peak level reached by the 10-year US
Treasury yield? For reference, the most recent intraday peak was 5.22%.

Interestingly, most respondents feel that the US will ultimately address its debt
burden through a combination of muddling through it indefinitely, followed by
inflating it away, and stronger economic growth. Fiscal consolidation is well
down the list with only default lower (overwhelmingly so) as an option.

## Document 345: The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Institute Employment Report_ September 2026 Bank of America Institute analysis shows slower payroll growth, declining unemployment payments and continued strength in lower-inco_223e3a6a.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13025075     1

Economy 
The Institute Employment Report: September 2026 
07 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Job growth softened further in September, according to Bank of America's deposit data, with growth of 1.4% year-over-year 
(YoY), from 1.5% in August. But falling unemployment payments in September indicate a relatively healthy overall picture. 
• Lower-income households' wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap. After-tax wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY 
for lower-income households in September, while rising to 3.8% and 3.7% for middle- and higher-income households, 
respectively. 
• Lower tax withholdings and a relative strengthening in the younger/lower-income end of the labor market are driving this story. 
Additionally, average hours have risen in some lower-paid industries, which is also boosting pay packets.

Job growth slowed in September, but unemployment trends are benign  
Our estimate of job growth – which uses Bank of America deposit data to track changes in the number of customer accounts 
receiving a paycheck (see Methodology) – slowed further in September, to 1.4% year-over-year (YoY) from 1.5% YoY in August. 
The data can be noisy, partly due to seasonal variation and differences in pay-period timing, but in our view, the latest reading is 
consistent with the easing in payrolls growth seen in the September Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (Exhibit 1). 
Still, despite slower hiring, there is little sign of a rise in joblessness in Bank of America data. Unemployment payments into 
customer accounts showed a YoY decline in September (Exhibit 2). So, overall, labor market trends appear resilient.   
Exhibit 1: September payroll growth showed further slowing in 
Bank of America deposit account data

(BLS) and Automatic Data Processing (ADP) (monthly, YoY)

After-tax wage growth cooled for lower-income, but rose for other cohorts 
Lower-income wage growth eased to 4.5% YoY in September from 4.7% YoY in August, while wage growth for middle- and 
higher-income households rose to 3.8% and 3.7% YoY, respectively. Overall, these changes were modest, and lower-income 
households are continuing to see faster after-tax wage growth than other cohorts, a notable turnaround from the “K-shaped” 
pattern we observed over much of 2025 and 2026 (Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: Lower-income wage growth remains strongest, despite a narrowing gap 
After-tax wage and salary growth by household income terciles, based on Bank of America aggregated consumer deposit account data (three-month moving 
average, YoY%, SA)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In our view, the pick-up in lower- and middle-income households’ after-tax wage growth continues to reflect the impact of the 
One Big Beautiful Bill Act (OBBA) in reducing tax withholdings, alongside an underlying rise in job-to-job changes at the 
younger/lower-income end of the labor market (for more, read: The Institute Employment Report: August 2026). Consistent with 
that underlying pick up, BLS data shows a solid YoY rise in average hours worked in several relatively lower-paid industries, such 
as manufacturing, retail and other services. Hourly workers in these industries are likely seeing rising paychecks as a result 
(Exhibit 4).
